# Joint family and its businesses — the Randhawa family (seven books)

Three brothers — **Amarjeet**, **Lakhbeer** and **Lakhwinder Randhawa** — keep a joint household. **Randhawa Joint Family** is the joint family's own book: the house, the gold, the deposits, the common kitchen, and one account for each sub-family — what the family spent for that household or gave it, less what the household put in, into the family or into a family business. A sub-family account is not a debt: at the year end it is settled or carried forward, as the family decides. Each brother's household keeps its own book. The family owns **Randhawa Agriculture** and **Randhawa Real Estate** outright and shares **Randhawa Brick Field** 3 : 2 with an outside partner, **Nirmaljeet Gill**; money a business sends home is the family's income. Money that moves between two books is written in both, in accounts named after the other book, and the family summary shows each pair agreeing.

| Who | Role in this example |
|:---|:---|
| Amarjeet Randhawa | Eldest brother — keeps the joint family's book and runs the farm; head of his household (with Indu Randhawa) |
| Lakhbeer Randhawa | Brother — runs the brick field; head of his household (with his son Aman Randhawa); took ₹50,000 from the family in August, to be returned |
| Lakhwinder Randhawa | Brother — a salaried teacher, looks after the real estate; head of his household (with Harjeet and Supreet Randhawa) |
| Aman Randhawa | Lakhbeer's son — runs the farm day to day; holds the farm-labour advance |
| Nirmaljeet Gill | Outside partner in the brick field — share 2 of 5 |

Amounts are in ₹; paise are shown only when there are paise. Plain words come first and the true side follows in small brackets — Money in (Dr), You will give (Cr) — so the side is never lost. Every figure is illustrative.

## Book: Randhawa Joint Family — joint family book

The joint family's own book · house, gold and deposits · the common kitchen · one account per sub-family, settled or carried forward · money put into the family businesses · their money sent home as family income.

### 2. Every account (ledger)

*Randhawa Joint Family · 01 Apr 2025 to 31 Mar 2026 · Kept on money paid and received, and what people owe; no year-end adjustments. · Year closed · Made on 02 Apr 2026, 10:00*

One account after another: date, entry number, the other account (*To …* on the Dr side, *By …* on the Cr side) with the entry's note in small type, the amount on its side, the running balance and its side. Accounts that carry a balance open with *Opening balance b/f* and end *Closing balance c/d · Total · Opening balance b/d*; income and expense accounts end with their total for the year. The last rows of the heads, the drawings and the own-money accounts are the year-close entries (numbers ending Y1, Y2 …) that the book makes itself on 31 Mar 2026 — they are listed in section 4.

#### 4.3 Year close — the entries the book makes itself on 31 Mar 2026

*Lakhwinder's Family · year ended 31 Mar 2026 · Kept on money paid and received, and what people owe; no year-end adjustments. · Year closed · Made on 02 Apr 2026, 10:00*

## Book: Randhawa Real Estate — family business book

Two shops let at Kotli Chowk · rent charged quarterly · a plot sold and another bought · tenants' deposits, one set off and forfeited when a tenant left owing · rent written off · an improvement added to the cost.

## Family summary — the books agree

*Randhawa Joint Family · Amarjeet's Family · Lakhbeer's Family · Lakhwinder's Family · Randhawa Agriculture · Randhawa Real Estate · Randhawa Brick Field · FY 2025-26 · Kept on money paid and received, and what people owe; no year-end adjustments. · Year closed · Made on 02 Apr 2026, 10:00*

Money that moves between two books is written in both, in accounts named after the other book. For each pair: the balances brought forward agree, and what one book gave is what the other received, entry for entry. A family and a sub-family also agree balance for balance on opposite sides. An owner and the business he owns agree entry by entry — money put in against the business's capital, money sent home against its drawings — but not in balance: the owner holds the business at the money put in, while the business's own money also carries its profit, so the difference is printed under the table as *profit kept in the business*.

| Book | Its accounts for the other book | Brought forward ₹ | Given (Dr) ₹ | Received (Cr) ₹ | Other book | Its accounts | Brought forward ₹ | Given (Dr) ₹ | Received (Cr) ₹ | Entries | Agree |
|:---|:---|---:|---:|---:|:---|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|:---:|
| Randhawa Joint Family | Amarjeet's Family A/c | 18,000 Dr | 99,000 | 1,17,000 | Amarjeet's Family | Randhawa Joint Family A/c | 18,000 Cr | 1,17,000 | 99,000 | 4 | Yes |
| Randhawa Joint Family | Lakhbeer's Family A/c | 0 | 2,68,500 | 50,000 | Lakhbeer's Family | Randhawa Joint Family A/c | 0 | 50,000 | 2,68,500 | 7 | Yes |
| Randhawa Joint Family | Lakhwinder's Family A/c | 12,000 Cr | — | 1,80,000 | Lakhwinder's Family | Randhawa Joint Family A/c | 12,000 Dr | 1,80,000 | — | 3 | Yes |
| Randhawa Joint Family | Randhawa Agriculture A/c<br>Income from Randhawa Agriculture A/c | 64,18,100 Dr | 80,000 | 7,50,000 | Randhawa Agriculture | Capital A/c – Randhawa Joint Family<br>Drawings A/c – Randhawa Joint Family | 64,18,100 Cr | 7,50,000 | 80,000 | 3 | Yes |
| Randhawa Joint Family | Randhawa Real Estate A/c<br>Income from Randhawa Real Estate A/c | 31,37,000 Dr | — | 3,10,000 | Randhawa Real Estate | Capital A/c – Randhawa Joint Family<br>Drawings A/c – Randhawa Joint Family | 31,37,000 Cr | 3,10,000 | — | 2 | Yes |
| Randhawa Joint Family | Randhawa Brick Field A/c<br>Income from Randhawa Brick Field A/c | 11,64,000 Dr | 3,20,000 | 3,30,000 | Randhawa Brick Field | Randhawa Joint Family A/c | 11,64,000 Cr | 3,30,000 | 3,20,000 | 5 | Yes |

**Profit kept in the business — Randhawa Joint Family and Randhawa Agriculture:** the business's own money after the year close ₹71,94,265, less the money put in that Randhawa Joint Family holds ₹64,98,100, is ₹6,96,165. That is the business's profit for Randhawa Joint Family ₹14,46,165 less the ₹7,50,000 taken out (₹14,46,165 − ₹7,50,000 = ₹6,96,165).

**Profit kept in the business — Randhawa Joint Family and Randhawa Real Estate:** the business's own money after the year close ₹33,59,500, less the money put in that Randhawa Joint Family holds ₹31,37,000, is ₹2,22,500. That is the business's profit for Randhawa Joint Family ₹5,32,500 less the ₹3,10,000 taken out (₹5,32,500 − ₹3,10,000 = ₹2,22,500).

**Profit kept in the business — Randhawa Joint Family and Randhawa Brick Field:** the business's own money after the year close ₹13,63,520, less the money put in that Randhawa Joint Family holds ₹14,84,000, is −₹1,20,480. That is the business's profit for Randhawa Joint Family ₹2,09,520 less the ₹3,30,000 taken out (₹2,09,520 − ₹3,30,000 = −₹1,20,480).

How to read it. **Sub-families:** each household's account in the joint book and the joint-family account in the household's book move together; Amarjeet's was settled on 31 Mar 2026 (the family bore it), Lakhbeer's ₹50,000 was returned through the brick field and from his share of the business income, and the rest is carried forward. **Family businesses:** the joint book holds each business at the money the family put in; money a business sends home is the family's income (*Income from …*). Each business works out its own profit in its own book and it is not written in the joint book: Randhawa Agriculture ₹14,46,165, Randhawa Real Estate ₹5,32,500, and the family's 3 of 5 of the brick field's, ₹2,09,520.
